## Document 012: 115年10月賦稅收入初步統計 (2026-11-11)

下次發布日期：115 年 12 月 11 日下午 4 時

聯 絡 人 ： 陳 巧 芸 科 長 
電   話：02-2322-7555 
115年 10月賦稅收入初步統計 
一、整體賦稅收入 
（一）實徵淨額 
10 月實徵淨額 5,669 億元，較上年同月增加 1,495 億元；累計 1 至
10 月實徵 4 兆 1,383 億元，較上年同期增加 8,499 億元(+25.8%)，
主要由證交稅、營所稅及營業稅所貢獻。隨新版財政收支劃分法實
施，營業稅劃歸中央政府比率由 61.2%降為 4.5%(獲配稅額隨之減
少 2,662 億元)，致中央政府稅收增加數 3,906 億元(+15.8%)，不及
地方政府增加數 4,671 億元(+68.5%)。 
（二）預算達成率 
累 計1 至 10 月 實 徵 淨 額 占 全 年 預 算 數 91.4% ， 其 中 中 央 政 府 為
92.8%，地方政府為87.7%；與全年預算數差距分別為3,913億元、
2,227億元、1,614億元。 
圖 1 賦稅實徵淨額及年增率

說明：115 年 5-7 月年增率跳動較大，係因 114 年所得稅結算申報及繳納期限延長至 6 月底所致。

二、主要稅目別變動
（一）10 月實徵淨額與上年同月比較，以營利事業所得稅增加 1,133 億元、
證券交易稅增加 220 億元及綜合所得稅增加 120 億元較多。
（二）累計 1 至 10 月實徵淨額與上年同期比較，以證券交易稅增加 3,297
億元、營利事業所得稅增加 2,986 億元及營業稅增加 1,272 億元較多。
圖 2 主要稅目別變化
(115 年 1-10 月)
1.營利事業所得稅
(1)10 月實徵淨額 4,144 億元，較上年同月增加 1,133 億元(+37.6%)，
主因暫繳稅款成長。
(2)累計 1 至 10 月實徵淨額 1 兆 4,172 億元，較上年同期增加 2,986
億元(+26.7%)，主因結算申報自繳、暫繳稅款俱增及延分期稅款
陸續入帳。
2.綜合所得稅
10 月實徵淨額 614 億元，較上年同月增加 120 億元(+24.3%)，主因
扣繳稅款增加所致；累計 1 至 10 月實徵淨額 8,894 億元，較上年同
期增加 910 億元(+11.4%)，主因結算申報自繳稅額增加，以及盈餘分

配、薪資所得等扣繳稅款均呈成長。
3.營業稅
(1)10月受進口代徵稅額增加及適用外銷零稅率營業人申請退稅增加
之交互影響，營業稅實徵淨額為負77億元，較上年同月(負146億元)
增加69億元。
(2)累 計1 至10 月 實 徵 淨額6,153 億 元 ， 較 上年 同 期 增 加1,272 億元
(+26.1%)，主因內需穩定，資通與視聽產品、電子零組件等進口
代徵稅額增加，惟外銷營業人申請退稅亦增，抵銷部分增幅。

### Page 3

3
4.貨物稅
10月實徵淨額73億元，較上年同月減少41億元(-35.6%)，累計1至10
月實徵淨額948億元，較上年同期減少248億元(-20.7%)，主因新購汽
機車及電動車之減免徵退稅增加，加以汽柴油減徵幅度擴大所致。
5.證券交易稅
(1)10 月及累計 1 至 10 月上市上櫃股票平均每日成交值為 1 兆 1,210
億元、1 兆 1,437 億元，分別較上年同期(6,267 億元、4,632 億元)
增 78.9%、1.5 倍。
(2)10 月證交稅實徵淨額 523 億元， 較上年同月增加 220 億元
(+72.6%)；累計 1 至 10 月實徵淨額 5,583 億元，較上年同期增加
3,297 億元(+1.4 倍)。
6.關稅
10 月實徵淨額 132 億元，較上年同月增加 7 億元(+6.0%)，累計 1 至
10 月實徵淨額 1,356 億元，較上年同期增加 88 億元(+6.9%)，均因機
械及電機設備、基本金屬、塑橡膠及其製品等進口稅額增加。
7.贈與稅
10 月實徵淨額 23 億元，較上年同月減少 11 億元(-31.9%)，主因大額

稅款案件減少；累計 1 至 10 月實徵淨額 234 億元，較上年同期增加
32 億元(+15.9%)，主因大額稅款案件增加。
8.土地增值稅
10 月實徵淨額 56 億元，較上年同月增加 3 億元(+5.9%)，主因大額
稅款案件增加；累計 1 至 10 月實徵淨額 560 億元，較上年同期減少
11 億元(-2.0%)，主因房地交易量及大額稅款案件減少。
9.使用牌照稅
10 月實徵淨額 6 億元，較上年同月減少 1 億元(-10.2%)，累計 1 至
10 月實徵淨額 676 億元，較上年同期減少 1 億元(-0.1%)，主因開徵
輛數減少，以及稅款入帳時間差異所致。
（三）前 10月主要稅目實徵淨額占全年預算數之比率：
1.大於 100%(已提前達成全年預算目標)者有遺產稅(123%)、贈與稅
(107%)、印花稅(106%)、房屋稅(105%)及營所稅(102%)。
2.低於 100%者有牌照稅(99%)、營業稅(94%)、綜所稅(93%)、契稅
(90%)、關稅(88%)、菸酒稅(81%)、證交稅(80%)、貨物稅(79%)、土

增稅(71%)及特銷稅(47%)等。

三、政府別賦稅收入概況
(一)中央政府
累計 1 至 10 月中央政府賦稅收入(不含中央統籌分配稅款)實徵淨
額 2 兆 8,579 億元(占全年預算數 92.8%)，較上年同期增加 3,906 億元
(+15.8%)，主因新版財政收支劃分法施行，營業稅劃歸中央政府比率
大幅調降，獲配稅額隨之減少 2,662 億元，惟證交稅、所得稅分別增加
3,297 億元、3,306 億元，互抵後呈現上升。
(二)地方政府
累計 1 至 10 月地方政府賦稅
收入(含中央統籌分配稅款)實
徵淨額 1 兆 1,487 億元(占全年
預算數 87.7%)，較上年同期增
加 4,671 億元(+68.5%)，主因
納入中央統籌分配之營業稅
增加 4,013 億元所致。
(三)中央特種基金
累計 1 至 10 月撥入中央特種基金之健康福利捐實徵淨額 220 億元，較
上年同期增加 4 億元(+1.7%)；房地合一課徵所得稅撥入住宅基金 144
億元，較上年同期減少 6 億元(-4.0%)；撥入長照基金 953 億元(其中 575

億元來自房地合一課徵所得稅、220 億元來自菸稅，158 億元來自遺贈
稅)，較上年同期增加 2 億元(+0.2%)。

廣達(2382 TT) Trading Buy

AI 伺服器展望維持不變，投資建議 Trading Buy 
投資建議：廣達目前未見主要 CSP 客戶調整 AI 伺服器訂單，
且考慮到 CSP 對於記憶體、CPU 和 PCB 等關鍵零組件的鎖料
能力，我們預期 Vera Rubin 將於如原先預期於 1Q27 放量，2027
年營運展望維持正向，然而考慮到近期市場對於資料中心建置
的疑慮升溫而可能抑制評價，因此我們給予廣達 Trading Buy 的
投資建議，目標價 324 元。 
3Q26 營收 1.12 兆元，稅後 EPS 6.92 元：3Q26 營收 1.12 兆元，
QoQ+7.6%；毛利率 4.75%， QoQ-0.27ppts；營業利益 322.04 億
元，QoQ-6.0%；歸屬母公司淨利 284.22 億元，QoQ-0.8%；稅後
EPS 6.92 元。 
PC 市場需求疲弱，伺服器業務支撐營收季增： 3Q26 筆電出貨
960 萬台，QoQ-17%，考慮到筆電零組件漲價帶動 ASP 提高，
我們預估 PC 營收季減高個位數。通用型伺服器營收優於預期，
除 2H26 AI Agent 強勁的需求外，ASP 亦由於零組件漲價而提
升。AI 伺服器部分出貨量較 2Q26 微幅下滑，但同樣受零組件
漲價帶動 ASP 提升影響，預估 AI 伺服器營收仍季增低個位數。 
2027 年伺服器展望不變：近期市場對於限制資料中心相關法案
的擔憂升溫，然而廣達表示目前客戶方面並未調整訂單，就現
階段來看主要 CSP 的資本支出尚未放緩，我們預估 2027 年 AI
伺服器展望將維持不變。我們預期 1Q27 Vera Rubin 將可如先
前預期放量，帶動 2027 年 AI 伺服器營收高雙位數增長。考慮
到 AI Agent 的需求持續成長，預期 2027 年通用型伺服器全年
營收呈雙位數年增。 
預估 2027 年營收 5.99 兆元，稅後 EPS 30.85 元：群益預估 2027
年合併營收 5.99 兆元，YoY+44.1%；毛利率 4.18%，YoY-0.6ppts；
營業利益 1,490.08 億元，YoY+21.5%，營益率 2.49%， YoY-
0.5ppts；歸屬母公司淨利 1,266.99 億元，YoY+18.3%，稅後 EPS 
30.85 元。

### Page 2

個股報告 
                                        2026 年 11 月 12 日

2 群益投顧 
  ESG 
廣達積極推動環境、社會及公司治理（ESG）永續發展，並將節能減碳、人
才培育及供應鏈管理納入企業營運策略。環境方面，公司於 2024 年通過科
學基礎減碳目標倡議（SBTi）審查，承諾以 2022 年為基準，2030 年前將範
疇一及範疇二溫室氣體排放量降低 42%、範疇三降低 25%，並以 2050 年達
成淨零排放為長期目標。公司亦持續推動再生能源使用及能源管理，2024 年
綠電使用占總用電量 47.51%，並設定 2030 年再生能源使用比例達 50%的目
標。 
社會責任方面，廣達重視員工權益、職業安全及人才培育，遵循責任商業聯
盟（ RBA）行為準則，並透過廣達文教基金會及廣藝基金會推動教育與藝文
活動。公司治理方面，廣達持續強化風險管理、資訊安全及供應鏈永續管理，
並參照 TCFD 及 SASB 等國際框架揭露永續資訊，以提升營運透明度及長期
經營韌性。 
公司簡介 
廣達電腦（Quanta Computer Inc.， 2382 TT）成立於 1988 年，總部位於台灣
桃園，為全球主要電子產品原廠設計製造（ODM）廠商之一，主要業務涵蓋
筆記型電腦、伺服器、雲端運算設備及其他智慧電子產品。公司早期以筆記
型電腦代工為主要業務，憑藉產品研發、供應鏈管理及全球製造能力，建立
與國際品牌客戶的長期合作關係，近年則積極擴展雲端運算及人工智慧相
關業務。 
廣達透過旗下雲達科技（QCT）布局資料中心市場，提供伺服器、儲存設備、
網路交換器及整機櫃解決方案，並隨大型雲端服務供應商（CSP）擴 大 AI 基
礎建設投資，逐步深化 AI 伺服器業務布局。相較傳統筆電代工，AI 伺服器
對系統整合、散熱、電源管理及高速互連技術要求較高，帶動產品複雜度及
單機價值提升，成為公司營收成長的重要驅動因素。 
展望未來，廣達持續推進 AI 伺服器及機櫃級系統整合能力，並透過全球生
產據點布局，強化供應鏈韌性及在地化交付能力。隨 AI 運算需求持續擴張，
伺服器業務可望維持公司中長期成長動能，惟仍須關注 CSP 資本支出、產
品轉換時程及新平台量產效率對營運的影響。 
3Q26 營收 1.12 兆元，稅後 EPS 7.08 元：3Q26 營收 1.12 兆元，QoQ+7.6%，
YoY+125.2%；毛利率 4.75%，QoQ-0.27ppts，YoY-2.10ppts；營業利益 322.04
億元，QoQ-6.0%，YoY+75.3%，營益率 2.89%，QoQ-0.41ppts，YoY-0.82ppts；
歸屬母公司淨利 284.22 億元，QoQ-0.8%， YoY+73.0%；稅後 EPS 7.08 元。
PC 市場需求疲弱，伺服器業務支撐營收季增： 3Q26 筆電出貨 960 萬台，
QoQ-17%， YoY-24%，出貨量略優於 2Q26 預期的 925 萬台，考慮到筆電零
組件漲價帶動 ASP 提高，我們預估 PC 營收季減高個位數。通用型伺服器營
收優於預期，主要原因除 2H26 AI Agent 強勁的需求外，亦由於 ASP 受零
組件漲價影響而提升。AI 伺服器部分出貨量較 2Q26 微幅下滑，但同樣受零
組件漲價帶動 ASP 提升影響，預估 AI 伺服器營收仍季增低個位數。 
零組件漲價及產品組合改變導致毛利率承壓：由於(1)筆電持續因成本上揚
而壓縮獲利，且出貨數下滑不利經濟規模(2)雖 AI 伺服器出貨量季減，但由

### Page 3

個股報告 
                                        2026 年 11 月 12 日

3 群益投顧 
ASP 提升驅動營收增長，造成毛利率稀釋(3)通用型伺服器出貨量小幅季增，
且 ASP 提升。綜上原因導致 3Q26 毛利率季減 0.27ppts，帶動營益率季減
0.41ppts，此一情景預估將延續至 2027 年，且我們預估 Vera Rubin 世代平
台 ASP 較 GB300 系列接近倍增，雖營收與獲利將可持續上升，但未來 margin
將持續承壓。雖目前有數個專案採用 Consignment model，然而主要以通用
型伺服器與 ASIC 平台為主，且出貨形式由 L6 至 L11 皆有，營收貢獻相對
於採用 B/S 的整櫃 GPU 伺服器而言佔比極低。據我們了解目前已有確定一
個 Vera Rubin 的大型專案及數個規模較小的專案將採用 Consignment 
model，但預估占整體伺服器營收比重仍低，對於毛利率的改善效果有限。
由於 B/S 將稀釋毛利率，Consignment 則會使營收下滑，因此未來針對 ODM
的獲利能力的評估重點應轉向以營業利益年複合成長率(CAGR)或是 ROIC
為主。 
終止華亞科技園區收購，2028 年產能擴充計畫維持不變：廣達表示，由於
對廠方規格及交付時程等事項無法取得共識，因此撤銷友達華亞科技園區
廠房的收購案，原先規劃的產能將透過海外投資計畫或其他彈性應變方案，
維持 2028 年投產的計畫不變。廣達 3Q26 資本支出 128 億元，2026 前三季
累計資本支出達 346 億元，預估 2027 年資本支出介於 400-450 億元，投資
金額成長幅度收斂，將主要用於改良租賃廠房及電力升級。管理層表示 AI
擴廠計畫為配合 CSP 客戶未來 12-24 個月的需求規劃，且伺服器屬於客製
化接單生產(Build-to-Order)，非承擔庫存風險的標準品倍或生產，資本支出
主要投入的項目為廠房電力設施及高階冷卻系統等通用的基礎設施，因此
縱然單一客戶或是特定世代的 AI 伺服器平台需求放緩，亦可無縫轉換產能，
較不會出現過去供過於求時，閒置產能導致的折舊與維護費用等沉沒成本，
營運風險相對較小。 
2027 年伺服器展望不變：美國期中選舉後，市場對於限制資料中心相關法
案的擔憂升溫，然而廣達表示目前客戶方面並未調整訂單。廣達的客戶以五
大 CSP 為主，Neocloud 的占比不高，就現階段來看主要 CSP 的資本支出尚
未放緩，我們預估 2027 年 AI 伺服器展望將維持不變。3Q26 AI 伺服器出貨
量的下滑主要原因為新舊平台交替的陣痛期，預估 4Q26 伺服器出貨量仍將
下滑，但因 Vera Rubin 相較 GB300 近乎倍增的 ASP，AI 伺服器營收將維持
季增。此外，目前在記憶體、PCB 及 CPU 缺貨問題對於消費性產品及通用
型伺服器影響較大，但由於廣達至 2027 年的訂單人以五大 CSP 為主，且
CSP 巨頭鎖料能力較強，因此 AI 伺服器的長短料問題相較之下較輕微，我
們預期 1Q27 Vera Rubin將可如先前預期放量，帶動 2027 年 AI 伺服器營收
高雙位數增長。通用型伺服器部分，受惠 AI Agent 的應用落地帶動通用型
伺服器需求增長，預估 4Q26 通用型伺服器將持續季增。考慮到 AI Agent 的
需求持續成長，預期 2027 年通用型伺服器除 Share gain 外，亦受惠整體市
場需求提升帶來的成長，全年營收呈雙位數年增。 
管理層預期 2027 年筆電市場持續低迷：管理層預期 2027 年筆電需求將持
續受料件漲價及缺料等問題壓抑，導致 2027 年筆電出貨量下滑高個位數至
低雙位數左右，考慮到目前筆電營收占比已不足 15%，因此我們認為 2027
年合併營收將受伺服器的強勁成長帶動而年增。 
預估 4Q26 營收 1.19 兆元，稅後 EPS 7.19 元：預期 4Q26 筆電市場仍舊低
迷，AI 伺服器處於世代交替的轉換期，出貨量將持續下滑，但受 ASP 提升
影響而帶動 AI 伺服器營收季增，此外通用型伺服器出貨亦維持強勁。綜上

4 群益投顧 
所述，群益預估 4Q26 營收 1.19 兆元，QoQ+7.0%：毛利率 4.62%， QoQ-
0.13ppts，毛利續持續下滑主要受到 AI 伺服器 ASP 提升帶來的稀釋，以及
筆電出貨量下滑造成的規模經濟效果衰減所影響；營業利益 331.68 億元，
QoQ+3.0%，營益率 2.78%；歸屬母公司淨利 288.35 億元，QoQ+1.45%；稅
後 EPS 7.02 元。 
預估 2027 年營收 5.99 兆元，稅後 EPS 30.85 元：群益預估 2027 年合併營
收 5.99 兆元，YoY+44.1%；毛利率 4.18%，YoY-0.6ppts；營業利益 1,490.08
億元，YoY+21.5%，營益率 2.49%， YoY-0.5ppts；歸屬母公司淨利 1,266.99
億元，YoY+18.3%，稅後 EPS 30.85 元。 
投資建議：廣達目前未見主要 CSP 客戶調整 AI 伺服器訂單，且考慮到 CSP
對於記憶體、CPU 和 PCB 等關鍵零組件的鎖料能力，我們預期 Vera Rubin
將於如原先預期於 1Q27 放量，帶動 2027 年 AI 伺服器營收維持高雙位數成
長。通用型伺服器則受惠於 AI Agent 帶動運算需求及市占提升，預估全年
營收呈雙位數年增。預期伺服器強勁的成長將可抵銷筆電市場的低迷，帶動
2027 年營收年增，惟 Vera Rubin 的高單價將使毛利率及營益率承壓，但營
業利益亦將仍持續增長，整體營運展望維持正向，然而考慮到近期市場對於
資料中心建置的疑慮升溫而可能抑制評價，因此我們給予廣達 Trading Buy
的投資建議，目標價 324。

  評等 定義 
  強力買進(Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間≥ 35%  
  買進(Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<35%  
  區間操作(Trading Buy) 5%≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<15%

中立(Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等 
預期近期股價將處於盤整 
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】 
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。  
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。 
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」、「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。 
停止推薦情境： 
1. 達目標價。 
2. 雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。 
3. 推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】 
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

### Page 1

Taiwan Consumer Discretionary

What’s new: Giant announced its 3Q26 results after market hours on 9 
November 2026. We reaffirm our Buy (1) rating on its improving 
fundamentals and the recovery of the global bike market. We believe 
inventory destocking is coming to an end.

What’s the impact: 3Q26 results: missed on operating margin. Giant’s 
3Q26 revenue of TWD16.311bn (-2.1% QoQ, +6.2% YoY) was roughly in 
line with our estimate and the Bloomberg consensus. Its gross margin of 
23.9% beat our estimate and the Bloomberg consensus by 2.9pp and 
1.1pp, respectively, driven by product mix optimisation and new product 
launches in its own brand. However, its operating margin of 3.1% missed 
our estimate and the consensus by 1.4pp and 2.1pp, respectively, due to 
marketing activities for MY27 launches and legal fees related to the WRO. 
Thus, its 3Q26 operating profit of TWD510m (-49.0% QoQ, +28.9% YoY) 
missed our estimate and the consensus by 31.3% and 42.2%, respectively. 
Its 3Q26 net profit of TWD330m (-48.0% QoQ, -5.7% YoY) also missed our 
estimate and the consensus by 39.6% and 47.8%, respectively. Its 3Q26 
EPS finished at TWD0.84.

Demand recovery in Europe and China; a bright 2027. Giant sees 
gradual market recovery in Europe on new model launches and demand 
recovery in China. In the US, Giant continues to work towards having the 
WRO lifted, but will adjust production allocation to maximise the supply to 
meet demand in the US market. Its E-bike revenue accounted for 21% of 
9M26 revenue, which we believe reflects the declining OEM segment. 
Giant aims for its e-bike shipments to decline by 1% YoY, from 516,000 
units in 2025 to 510,000 in 2026, on weaker OE customer demand. For 
2026, it guides for sales to decline by low-single-digit % YoY, with the gross 
margin improving to 21-23% (from 20-21%) and an operating margin of 1-
3%. We lower our 2026-28E EPS by 5-30% after factoring in the 3Q26 
results with more conservative operating-margin estimates on marketing 
expenses.

What we recommend: We reaffirm our Buy (1) call with an unchanged 
12M TP of TWD121, still based on our 1-year forward EPS forecast with an 
unchanged target PER of 22x to reflect its improved earnings trajectory, 
and we look for a better year in 2027. Key downside risks: slower-than-
expected e-bike penetration in the EU and macro/tariff uncertainties.

How we differ: Our 2026-28E EPS are 8-26% below the consensus, likely 
as our revenue and operating margin forecasts are more conservative.

9 November 2026

3Q26 results review: missed on operating margin

➢ 3Q26 net profit missed our estimate and the consensus by 39-48% 
➢ Better-than-expected recovery in Europe and China; a brighter 2027 
➢ Reaffirming our Buy (1) rating with an unchanged 12M TP of TWD121

Company profile 
Founded in 1972 and listed in 1994 on Taiwan’s main board, Giant is the largest bicycle maker in 
Taiwan, with an annual capacity of c.5m bicycles. In 2025, Asia accounted for 57% of its revenue, 
followed by Europe (33%) and America (7%). Around 74% of its 2025 revenue came from OBM (Giant, 
Liv, momentum) brands and 26% from OEM/ODM (Trek, Scott and Colnago) brands.

### Page 5

5

Giant Manufacturing (9921 TT): 9 November 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 7 Aug 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
The CEO and Chairperson are not the same individual. Led by the Liu family, there are 11
members on the board. The founder and former Chairman is now a director, with his niece, Mrs.
Tu, leading Giant. We view Mrs. Tu's leadership to be strong, as she is well-recognised by the
former Chairman and the board for her capabilities, and she is one of the few female
chairpersons to lead a Taiwanese company.
Capital management 2 Giant has maintained its dividend payout ratio between 50% and 100% over the past decade , which we believe to be reasonable considering its global leadership status and expansion needs.
Related party &
transaction 2
Revenue from related-party transactions accounted for 58-62% of total revenue over 2019-25,
with sales mostly from its OBM (ASP varies with products/markets). We observed no suspicious
activity.
S Product quality & safety 1
According to Giant's 2023 ESG report, Giant as the global OEM/OBM leader in the bicycle
industry, product quality and safety are the top priority. We see ongoing R&D expenses and
relevant investment in new models and note that Giant has introduced its "Right Ride" system to
assist customers seeking suitable bike options.
S Supply chain management 1
Giant has developed its Giant production management system that diversifies its capacity
globally and utilises production resources fully to lower political or regional risk. As Giant has
control over its material sourcing, design process and manufacturing/OEM, we believe it has
solid supply chain management capabilities that allow for better cost control than peers.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

## Document 126: 261110_citi_avc

### Page 1

Asia Vital Components (3017.TW)
3Q26 Beat on Margins Reinforces AI Thermal Ramp; TP Maintained at 
NT$4,350
CITI'S TAKE 
AVC reported net profit of NT$11.6bn, topping Coto/cons estimates by 
2%/2% respectively, driven by margins beat partly offset by a non-ops FX 
loss. GPM improvement was above expectations on a richer liquid cooling 
mix, yield gains and disciplined raw material pass-through. Mgmt. guided 
positively for 4Q26 and 2027, led by AI ASIC ramp-up and Vera Rubin ramp 
up. We model 16% QoQ revenue growth for 4Q26E. GPM guidance also 
points to further sequential improvement on a higher liquid cooling mix and 
scale effects. Mgmt. reiterated 2026E/27E capex of NT$15bn/15-20bn, 
alongside 50%+ cold plate capacity expansion, underscoring capacity 
conviction. We fine-tune our earnings forecasts to reflect updated guidance 
and maintain TP at NT$4,350 (25x 2027E EPS). Reiterate Buy.
3Q26 results beat on margins — AVC’s reported 3Q26 net profit of NT$11.6bn 
(+20% QoQ/+52% YoY) beat Citi/cons forecast by 2%/2%, driven by better-than-
expected margins, partly offset by a non-ops loss. 3Q26 GPM came in at 33.9% 
(+1.3ppts QoQ/+7.7 ppts YoY), above both Citi/cons estimates, mainly due to 
continued mix improvement, a higher liquid cooling contribution, yield gains, and 
disciplined pass-through of raw material price fluctuations to customers. Non-ops 
included a NT$304mn FX loss, which partially offset the strong core business.
ASIC ramp and capacity guidance underpin long-term outlook — Mgmt. guided 
positively for 4Q26 and 2027, led by AI ASIC project ramp-up and early Vera Rubin 
generation contribution. We now model 16% QoQ revenue growth for 4Q26E. GPM 
guidance also points to further sequential improvement vs. 3Q26, driven by a higher 
liquid cooling mix and scale effects. Mgmt. expects server compute tray liquid 
cooling penetration to reach 100% by end-2027. Mgmt. reiterated 2026E/26E capex 
guidance of NT$15bn/15-20bn, alongside 50%+ cold plate capacity expansion, 
reinforcing confidence in capacity build-out.
Implications — We fine-tune our 2026E-28E earnings forecasts to reflect updated 
guidance and outlook. We maintain our TP at NT$4,350, still based on 25x 2027E EPS. 
We believe AVC’s solid supply position across key customers’ AI GPU and ASIC projects, 
combined with a sustained growth trajectory, continues to support its valuation. With 
visibility extending into 2027, we see limited near-term read-through risk from the 
ongoing platform transition. We reiterate our Buy rating on AVC.

Bull/Bear: Asia Vital Components (3017.TW)

### Page 6

Asia Vital Components (3017.TW)
10 November 2026 Citi Research
6
Asia Vital Components
Company description
Asia Vital Components (AVC), founded in 1991, is a global leader in thermal
solutions, originally focusing on PCs but now specializing in servers and
networking. With Furukawa Electric (Japan) as its largest shareholder, AVC
provides a comprehensive range of cooling components, including vapor
chambers (VCs), fans, cold plates, CDUs, and manifolds, as well as chassis

and assembly services for PCs and servers, enabling holistic thermal solution
designs and one-stop shop services. AVC's diverse product portfolio supports
various sectors, including consumer IT, cloud infrastructure, transportation,
energy and networking.
Investment strategy
We rate AVC a Buy. AVC is well-positioned for the liquid-cooling transition,
with leadership in cold plates and broad participation across Nvidia and CSP
projects. Its high-volume machining capability, manufacturing uniformity,

and Vietnam-based capacity provide it with a defensible position as
Blackwell and future deployments expand. The company captures a wider
share of the thermal BOM, across coldplates, manifolds, QDs, and CDU
opportunities, providing a multi-layered earnings engine.
Valuation
We value AVC using a PE-based approach that factors in earnings momentum
and the expansion of its addressable market. Our TP of NT$4,350 is derived
from applying a 25x PE multiple on 2027E EPS, which we believe is justified

given the company’s entrenched positioned in the server thermal segment,
rising contribution from liquid cooling, and the increasing value of thermal
content per server as power density continues to climb. With these structural
drivers still in place, we expect the multiple to hold at a premium relative to its
historical averages.
Risks
This stock is High Risk based upon our quantitative model but assigning a
High Risk rating is not supported by other qualitative factors such as cold-

plate leadership and deep CSP penetration, and so a High Risk rating has not
been applied. Key downside risks to our investment thesis and target price
include: 1) slower adoption pace for liquid cooling adoption; 2) heightened
price competition; 3) lower-than-expected share in liquid cooling
components; and 4) emerging alternative cooling technologies.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.

call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in

catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

Micro-Star (2377.TW)
3Q26 Beat on Margins from ASP Pass-Through and Richer Mix
CITI'S TAKE 
MSI reported 3Q26 net profit of NT$4.0bn (+34% QoQ/+138% YoY), 
beating Citi/cons by 27%/45% on stronger-than-expected margins and 
non-op gains. GPM of 17.0% (+1.1ppts QoQ/+5.8 ppts YoY) topped Citi/cons 
at 15.8%/14.5%, as ASP hikes passed through rising component costs while 
moving-avg inventory costs lagged, alongside a richer high-end product 
mix. For 4Q26E, we expect further ASP increases and a rising server 
contribution to largely offset seasonal shipment declines. Into 2027, we 
expect PC demand to stay weak and pass-through room to narrow as 
memory price increases moderately. We raise 2026E-28E earnings and our 
TP to NT$145 (10x 2027E EPS). We stay Neutral as the share price already 
reflects the pass-through upside.
3Q26: Margin-led beat on cost pass-through and better mix — MSI reported 
3Q26 net profit of NT$4.0bn (+34% QoQ/+138% YoY), beating Citi/cons forecasts 
by 27%/45%, mainly on stronger-than-expected margins and non-op gains. GPM 
of 17.0% (+1.1ppts QoQ/+5.8ppts YoY) came in above Citi/cons at 15.8%/14.5%. 
Mgmt. attributed the upside to 1) ASP increase, as MSI raised prices in line with 
suppliers’ cost increases while its moving-average inventory costs rose less; and 2) 
a higher mix of high-end products. Non-op gains, including FX gains of over 
NT$100mn, further boosted the bottom line.
Price hikes to continue; 2027 PC market to keep shrinking — We reiterate our view 
that 4Q26 shipments will decline seasonally from the 3Q26 peak. However, mgmt. 
expects rising raw material costs and continued pass-through to keep lifting ASPs, 
offsetting lower volumes and making the seasonal decline milder than in prior years. 
The server business should also contribute more, potentially reaching a high-single-
digit revenue share. Into 2027, we expect weak PC demand amid elevated costs, 
with MSI’s MB and PC shipments declining by over 10%. Pass-through room should 
narrow as memory price increases moderately, though margins should stay elevated 
for a while, as mgmt. expects memory supply to stay tight in the coming quarters.
Implications — We raise our 2026E-28E earnings forecasts by 6-27%, reflecting 
sustained strong component cost pass-through and higher margin assumptions. 
We adjust TP to NT$145 (from NT$140), implying 10x 2027E EPS (from 12x). We 
believe the share price already reflects MSI’s opportunity to pass component costs 
through to end products, and we expect limited upside going forward, given that the 
PC demand recovery remains highly uncertain. Maintain Neutral.

### Page 6

Micro-Star (2377.TW)
13 November 2026 Citi Research
6
Micro-Star
Company description
Established in 1986 and listed in 1998, Taiwan-based MSI is a leading
OBM/ODM for gaming PCs, motherboards, graphic cards, industrial PCs, and
servers. MSI started business as a motherboard manufacturer but
transformed itself to become a leading gaming PC play in 2012 with a
comprehensive product portfolio across the gaming system and components.
MSI enjoys about 16% global share in the gaming NB industry, and is ranked

No.3 in graphic card shipments and No.3 in motherboard shipments globally.
Investment strategy
We rate MSI shares at Neutral as we anticipate earnings upward pressure
from the delince in PC and MB/VGA shipments in the near-term. While the
soaring component costs could be partially offset by price hike, we expect
muted demand and AI resource crowding to wiegh on margins upside. Further
upside maintains limited, though, until we see a meaningful recovery in core

gaming demand or a more substantial contribution from the server business.
Valuation
Our target price of NT$145 is based on 10x 2027E EPS, below the stock's five-
year average forward 12-month PE. This valuation reflects the earnings
downside risks from muted PC demand and margin pressure from soaring
component costs, while accounting for the time required for the server
business to achieve meaningful scale.
Risks
Key upside risks that could cause MSI's shares to trade above our target price

include: 1) a sharper-than-expected recovery in high-end gaming demand;
and 2) faster-than-anticipated revenue contribution from the AI server
segment. Key downside risks include: 1) a deeper-than-expected contraction
in global PC shipments; 2) prolonged supply shortages for gaming GPUs due
to Ai resource crowding; and 3) inability to pass through surging memory and
CPU costs to price-sensitive consumers.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

3Q26 Review: 4 분기 실적 반등 기대감 Up  
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 600,000 원(유지) 
 현재주가 292,500 원(10/29)

 동사의 3분기 실적은 매출액 45.1조원(-3.4%yoy), 영업이익 2.68조원
(+5.5%yoy, OPM 5.9%), 지배이익 1.7조원(-24.6%yoy)을 기록하며 시장 
컨센서스를 상회. 도매 판매 감소, 원가 부담 증가 영향 있었으나 판매보증
충당금 축소와 원가 절감 활동을 통해 수익성 방어 
 실적 증감 요인: 영업이익 증감 요인은 물량 -1.11조원, 믹스/인센티브 -
0.57조원, 금융 -0.11조원, 환율 +1.28조원, 기타 +0.64조원 등. 원자재 
가격 상승으로 5천억원의 비용 증가 요인 있었으나 위기 대응 원가 절감 
활동으로 대부분 상쇄할 수 있었음. 전분기 대비 기말환율 급락함에 따라 
판매보증충당금 지난 해 1.26조원에서 340억원으로 약 1.23조원 감소. 판
보충 효과는 일회성에 그치겠지만 원가 절감 효과 컸던 점 긍정적  
 금융 부문: 금융부문 매출액은 전년비 2.8% 증가했지만 영업이익은 26% 
급감. 장기 금리 상승에 따른 이자 비용 증가와 기업금융 충당금 적립, 중고
차 관련 비용 확대가 수익성 악화의 주요 원인. 중고차 관련 비용은 EV 리
스 반납 차량의 매각 손실 확대에 기인한 것 
 4분기 전망: 동사는 국내 공장 정상화와 아반떼, 투싼, GV90, GV80 HEV 
등 주요 신차 효과를 통해 4분기 생산 확대와 주요 수출 시장 공급 회복이 
가능할 것으로 전망하였음. 원가 부담은 지속되나 물량 감소에 따른 영향에
서는 벗어날 수 있을 것. 원자재 가격 상승에 대응해 원가절감 프로그램 확
대와 신차 출시를 통한 인센티브 정상화를 통해 수익성 개선 지속할 계획 
 연간 가이던스 하향 조정: 글로벌 도매 판매 목표를 기존 416만대에서 
400만대로 하향 조정. 매출 성장률은 +1~2%, OPM 가이던스는 기존 
6.3~7.3%에서 5.5~6.5%로 하향 조정. 자동차 부문 투자 계획은 17.8조
원에서 16.0조원으로 축소. 핵심 R&D 투자는 유지하되 Capex 집행 시기 
및 우선 순위 조정할 계획 
 4분기 실적 반등 전망: 4분기 주요 신차(투싼, 아반떼, GV80 HEV, GV90) 
출시 본격화로 3분기까지의 부진을 만회할 수 있을 것으로 기대. 원가 부담 
은 비용 절감 활동을 통해 충분히 만회가 가능한 것으로 3분기 실적을 통
해 확인됨. 본격적인 주가 반등을 위해서는 판매 회복 이상의 주가 트리거 
필요할 것으로 보이나, 4분기 반전의 실마리가 나타나고 있다는 점에 주목. 
동사에 대한 투자의견 매수와 목표주가 60만원을 유지함

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에도 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

투자기간 및 투자등급/투자의견 비율

## Document 241: [207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 신규 공장이 채운 실적, 수주가 더할 모멘텀삼성바이오로직스(207940) 신규 공장이 채운 실적, 수주가 더_21961458

Source PDF: all report pdf/[207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 신규 공장이 채운 실적, 수주가 더할 모멘텀삼성바이오로직스(207940) 신규 공장이 채운 실적, 수주가 더_21961458.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-07
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
미국 록빌·5공장 가세로 3분기 기대치 부합 전망 
삼성바이오로직스의 2026년 3분기 연결 매출액은 1조 3,637억 원(+8.4% YoY), 
영업이익은 6,092억 원(-3.8% YoY, 영업이익률 44.7%)으로 시장 기대치에 부합할 
전망이다. 5월 파업에 따른 생산 차질에도 1~4공장(총 60.4만 리터)의 풀가동과 
신규 공장의 매출 기여가 외형 성장을 뒷받침할 것으로 예상한다. 미국 록빌 
공장(6만 리터)은 3분기부터 매출 기여가 본격화되고, 5공장(18만 리터)도 
인증용(PPQ) 배치 생산을 통해 매출이 확대될 전망이다. 2분기에는 미국 공장·5공장 
관련 비용이 선반영됐으나, 3분기에는 매출 증가에 따른 고정비 부담 완화로 
영업이익률이 전분기 대비 0.3%p 상승할 것으로 예상한다. 
항체 중심 CDMO에서 펩타이드로 확장 
삼성바이오로직스의 2026년 신규 수주는 약 1.2조 원으로 전년 수주액의 약 20% 
수준에 그치고 있으나, 연말부터 내년까지 수주 모멘텀은 점차 강화될 것으로 
판단한다. 2025년 하반기 이후 미국의 의약품 관세 불확실성이 완화되며 글로벌 
제약사와의 논의가 늘어나고 있어, 신규 수주 계약으로 이어질 것으로 기대된다. 
11월 유상증자 납입이 마무리되면 6공장(18만 리터) 착공과 스위스 폴리펩타이드 
인수가 본격화되며 중장기 성장 기대감도 높아질 전망이다. 특히 폴리펩타이드 
인수가 연내 완료되면 2027년부터 연결 실적에 반영되는 동시에, 항체 의약품 
중심의 CDMO 사업을 GLP-1 등 펩타이드로 확장하는 계기가 될 전망이다. 
목표주가 200만 원으로 하향, 수주 회복이 관건 
삼성바이오로직스의 목표주가를 209만 원에서 200만 원으로 하향한다. 유상증자 
신주 발행에 따른 주당가치 4.7% 희석 효과를 반영했다. 폴리펩타이드 인수 효과는 
거래완료 이후 실적 추정치에 반영할 예정이다. 노조 파업 불확실성이 주가에 
부담으로 작용할 수 있어 신규 수주 모멘텀의 회복이 필요한 시점이다. 
목표주가 (하향) 2,000,000원

동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 외부의 압력이나 부당한 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하여 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
동 자료는 기관투자가 또는 제3자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다. 
동 자료는 조사분석자료 작성에 참여한 외부인(계열회사 및 그 임직원등)이 없습니다. 
조사분석 담당자 및 배우자는 해당종목과 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 
동 자료에 언급된 종목의 지분율 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
당사는 상기 명시한 사항 외 고지해야 하는 특별한 이해관계가 없습니다.

종목명 담당자 담당자(배우자) 보유여부 1%이상 보유여부 유가증권 발행관련 계열사 관계여부 공개매수 사무취급 IPO 회사채 지급보증 중대한 이해관계 M&A 관련 수량 취득가 취득일 
해당 사항 없음

Topics  Loss‑making firms are on the rise in China's growth sectors

In China, the number of companies operating in the red is increasing. And the rise is particularly striking in growth 
sectors. One factor behind the increase is the intense competition among firms, which has led to a situation in which 
only the strongest thrive. Another is that many next‑generation growth companies, despite being viewed as having great 
potential, are losing money.

■ Loss‑making firms are on the rise in growth sectors 
In recent years, the number of companies in China's 
growth sectors that are operating in the red has 
increased markedly. The share of loss-making firms in 
all listed firms (below, "loss‑making‑firm ratio") rose 
sharply from 9.6% in 2019 to 26.9% in 2025. Looking 
at the industry distribution of loss‑making firms, from 
2019 to 2022, the ratio increased mainly in 
non‑manufacturing sectors such as real estate and 
accommodation and eating/drinking services, 
reflecting the property market downturn and strict 
stay‑at‑home measures implemented to curb the spread 
of COVID‑19. Later, from 2022 to 2025, the 
loss‑making-firm ratio surged in 
information/communications equipment and 
electronic parts/devices; general, production, and 
commercial machinery; electrical machinery; and 
chemicals. These four sectors alone accounted for 
around 60% of the overall increase. 
They are the principal growth sectors in Chinese 
manufacturing. This is evidenced by the fact that the 
growth rates of value added in all four sectors exceed 
the average for Chinese manufacturing. Many of the 
high‑tech manufacturing industries that the Chinese 
government has prioritized for development are 
included in these sectors. For example, 5G base 
stations and semiconductors fall under 
information/communications equipment and 
electronic parts/devices. And robots fall under general, 
production, and commercial machinery. Meanwhile, 
solar cells and batteries fall under electrical machinery, 
and biopharmaceuticals and organic chemical 
materials fall under chemicals.  
Looking at recent developments in the 
manufacturing of information/communications 
equipment and electronic parts/devices, the global 
communications standard began shifting from 4G 
(LTE) to 5G services in around 2019, and ChatGPT was released in November 2022. These developments 
have provided tailwinds for the growth of the sector. Recently, as it has become clear that ordinary 
consumers and companies can make use of AI services on a daily basis, demand for AI‑related devices and 
semiconductors, as well as data centers, has soared. In this environment, many firms are focusing on 
strengthening development, manufacturing, and sales, and the sector's value added has continued to 
increase, rising from 780 billion yuan in 2022 to 910 billion yuan in 2025. Yet over the same period, the 
number of loss‑making firms increased from 113 to 198, and the loss‑making‑firm ratio jumped from 17.2% 
to 30.1%. 
■ Competition among companies means only the strongest thrive 
One reason loss‑making firms are increasing despite operating in growth sectors is the intense 
competition among companies. The fight for market share has led to overheated investment competition

### Page 3

ASIA MONTHLY REPORT  October 2026, No.307

The Japan Research Institute, Limited  
Economics Department

2 
and price competition. In information/communications 
equipment and electronic parts/devices manufacturing, 
the rate of growth of non-current assets from 2022 to 
2025 (+35.4%) far exceeded the growth rate of value 
added (+16.9%). This indicates that supply capacity is 
expanding faster than demand. In addition, the 
consumer price index (CPI) increased by just 0.4% 
over the same period. Service prices rose 2.2%, 
reflecting wage increases, while goods prices fell 0.7% 
due to excess supply in manufacturing. 
Information/communications equipment prices 
decreased by 3.5%, larger than the drop for goods 
overall. 
As the selection of the fittest through competition 
among companies has progressed, profits have become 
concentrated at a small number of highly competitive 
firms. The share of profits earned by the top 1% of 
firms in information/communications equipment and electronic parts/devices manufacturing rose sharply 
from 24.0% in 2022 to 43.3% in 2025. Leading companies are simultaneously establishing positions as 
both "domestic champions" and "global champions."  
■ Next‑generation growth companies are viewed as having great potential even though they 
are losing money 
However, loss‑making firms cannot simply be lumped together and labeled as losers. Globally, investors 
have shown increasing interest recently in companies with high growth potential even if they are operating 
in the red, and China has a considerable number of such firms. 
In information/communications equipment and 
electronic parts/devices manufacturing, one out of 
every two loss‑making firms is a company attracting 
strong investor interest. The Shanghai Composite 
Index rose 28.5% from 3,089 points at the beginning of 
2023 to 3,969 points at the end of 2025. 20 of the 198 
loss‑making firms in 2025 were not yet listed in 2023, 
and if these are excluded, there were 178 companies 
operating in the red as of 2025. Of these 178 firms, 96 
(53.9%) saw stock‑price increases over the period 
2023-2025 that exceeded the rise in the Shanghai 
Composite Index.  
One characteristic of these 96 companies with strong 
relative stock‑price performance is their active 
investment in R&D and intangible assets. Their 
average R&D intensity (R&D investment / sales) in 
2022 was 9.6%, and their ratio of intangible assets to 
value added stood at 60.5%. Intangible assets include 
computer software, patents, copyrights, and 
relationships with customers and suppliers. In contrast, 
the 82 loss‑making firms with weak relative 
stock‑price performance had an R&D intensity of only 
6.5% and an intangible‑asset‑to‑value‑added ratio of just 38.9%. 
As the above has shown, the increase in loss‑making firms in China's growth sectors is notable, and the 
factors behind it include not only the selection of the fittest as a result of competition among companies but 
also the emergence of next‑generation growth firms that are viewed as having great potential. 
  (Shinichi Seki)

Topics  Climate change heightens the risk of growth stagnation in Asia

Climate change could elevate the risk of economic growth flatlining in the ASEAN zone and India by causing labor 
productivity growth and capital accumulation to stall. To mitigate or avert such a scenario, establishing a business 
environment and institutions that promote "adaptation investment" by both the public and private sectors is an urgent 
task.

■ Heavy‑rain and flood damage is becoming increasingly serious in Asia 
Huge downpours and floods 
resulting in human casualties have 
been a frequent occurrence in Asia in 
the past several years. Still fresh in the 
memory is the debris flow that 
occurred in August this year near the 
border between Nepal and China, 
which as of the time of writing has 
been recognized as a major disaster 
with thousands dead or missing. And 
in November of last year, Southeast 
Asia and South Asia saw 
record‑breaking torrential rains, 
resulting in numerous casualties, 
particularly in Indonesia and Sri 
Lanka. 
One factor that heightens the risks 
of such floods and heavy rains is 
climate change. As temperatures rise, 
the amount of water vapor in the 
atmosphere increases. Therefore, 
global warming could intensify 
torrential rainfall. Although such risk 
is shared across Asia, the way its 
effects are felt differs by region. 
Within Asia, the ASEAN zone and 
India are heavily affected by 
monsoons (seasonal winds), with 
many areas experiencing concentrated 
rainfall during the wet season. In such 
areas, warming can further intensify 
monsoon‑driven heavy rains, 
increasing the risks of floods and 
landslides.  
■ Climate change will delay the escape from the "middle‑income trap" 
Climate change not only results in human casualties by way of natural disasters, but may also undermine 
the long‑term economic growth potential of ASEAN and India through various channels. The Asian 
Development Bank (ADB) [2024] conducted a quantitative analysis of the extent to which climate change 
will weigh on Asian economies over the long term. Based on the IPCC's "very high GHG emissions" 
scenario (SSP5‑8.5), the analysis assumes a 4.7°C rise in the Earth's average temperature by 2100 relative 
to pre-industrial levels, and estimates the difference in real GDP between that and a hypothetical scenario 
without climate change. The results illustrate the long‑term impact on economic growth through declines 
in labor productivity, stagnation in capital accumulation, and downward pressure on consumption due to 
sea‑level rise, floods, heat waves, and other phenomena caused by climate change. 
An examination of the results reveals that in every country/territory, the pushdown effect on real GDP 
relative to the no‑climate‑change scenario expands over time, with 2035, 2050, and 2070 each seeing 
greater impact. The pushdown effect for ASEAN and India is −15.0% to −8.8% in 2050 and −30.2% to

4 
−18.1% in 2070, larger than the effects for China (2050: −4.8%, 2070: −10.3%) and Central Asian countries 
(2050: −1.7%, 2070: −3.8%). Within Asia, the ASEAN zone and India can be considered regions where the 
risk of economic stagnation due to climate change is relatively large.  
This may make it even more difficult for ASEAN countries and India to escape the "middle‑income trap." 
Although their income levels have risen, they still remain far from high‑income status. Indonesia, Malaysia, 
the Philippines, and Thailand became middle‑income countries in the 1990s or earlier, but have still not 
been able to join the groups of high‑income countries. Furthermore, Vietnam and India, which became 
middle‑income countries in the 2000s or later, have yet to enter a high‑growth trajectory like China once 
did. Climate change, by causing labor productivity growth to stall amid rising temperatures and companies 
to curtail investment due to more frequent disasters, may lead to income growth in the ASEAN zone and 
India being delayed further.  
■ Improving the environment to accelerate "adaptation investment" will be important 
To alleviate the pushdown effect of climate change on growth, expansion of "adaptation investment" by 
the public and private sectors is essential. Specifically, this could include improving working environments 
and promoting measures to lighten workloads and reduce headcounts in order to offset declines in labor 
productivity caused by rising temperatures, as well as developing infrastructure and strengthening facilities 
to prevent damage to production equipment from sea‑level rise and floods. However, ASEAN and India 
cannot be said to have sufficiently 
developed the foundations for public 
and private promotion of such 
adaptation investment.  
As part of its Global Adaptation 
Initiative (ND‑GAIN), the University of 
Notre Dame (U.S.) evaluates countries' 
ability to use investment to achieve 
climate‑change adaptation in the form of 
a "readiness" score with three 
components: economic readiness, 
governance readiness, and social 
readiness. Respectively, these 
components assess 1) whether a 
business environment conducive to 
adaptation investment by private-sector 
companies has been put in place, 2) 
whether the effectiveness of institutions

5 
and policies to support private-sector adaptation investment has been secured, and 3) whether tangible and 
intangible social infrastructure that promotes investment has been established. 
Looking at readiness in 2024, ASEAN countries and India generally rank in the middle to lower tiers 
globally, with a large gap between them and developed countries. Notably, the Philippines, India, and 
Thailand fall outside the top 100, indicating weak momentum for adaptation investment. Breaking down 
the scores, problems are especially evident in the economic and governance categories. India and Thailand 
have particularly low economic readiness, ranking 113th and 124th, respectively, and five countries 
(Malaysia not included) fall outside the top 100 for governance readiness. Given this situation, to accelerate 
private‑sector adaptation investment, India and Thailand in particular need to move swiftly to improve the 
financial environment to make it easier to procure necessary funds domestically and internationally, relax 
regulations to encourage the launch of new businesses, and reform labor markets to enable the smooth 
securing of human resources. In addition, for Asia as a whole, it will also be important to boost confidence 
in the institutional factors that enhance application of investment for adaptation by increasing policy 
stability, ensuring the effectiveness of legal systems, and curbing corruption.

(Tomohiro Hosoi)

## Document 310: daily story_UAA Under Armour_劉昃恩_20261109

Source PDF: all report pdf/daily story_UAA Under Armour_劉昃恩_20261109.pdf
Report source: line報告備份

Pages: 5

### Page 1

十一月 09, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R Powered by the EFA Platform 1

動態更新 運動用品 ‧美國

Under Armour 
(UAA.O/UAA US)

客流量下滑考驗 
重要訊息 
Under Armour 2Q FY27 (7-9 月；財年止於 3 月) 營運約當損平。儘管 FY27
營收年減中個位數之展望不變，管理層下調毛利率指引，反映考量油價、
運價和庫存清倉等衝擊迎面而來。 
評論及分析 
2Q FY27 營運保持損平。2Q FY27 每股盈餘 0.00 美元，符合預期。營收
12.4 億美元，年減幅度擴大至 7%，反映全產品/通路需求皆轉差。地區別
方面，北美、歐非中東及亞太平洋銷售分別年減 7%、9%、9%，僅拉美
為唯一成長市場，年增 3%。再加上通路、地區組合不利與折扣力道加大，
毛利率延續衰退走勢，年減 0.3、季減 7.1 個百分點至 47.0%，拖累營利率
至僅 1.2%，年減 0.1、季減 3.1 個百分點。 
產業困境。管理層觀察到歐美客流量下滑與同業促銷力道加大風險，預計
3Q FY27 營收將年減 8-9% (連三季惡化)，劣於市場共識之 4%衰退；其中，
北美及歐非中東將為風暴中心，分別年減低雙位數、高個位數，亞太則將
改善至約年持平。加上油價和運價上漲、庫存清倉與匯率不利等因素疊加
影響，FY27 毛利率指引下調至 47.2-47.2% (前次為 47.7-48.2%)，且年增
1.7-2.2 個百分點中約有 1.5 個百分點係來自美國退稅挹注。 
庫存去化策略。2Q FY27 庫存週轉天數年增 7%、但季減 17%至 149 天，
仍高於過去十年同期平均水準之 131 天，主因營運規模縮減凸顯在途存貨
較高。管理層表示正與合作工廠協商推遲部分發貨，同時考慮 2H FY27 從
批發端去化庫存，但將避開同業清倉甩賣的場所。公司計畫持續減少產品
SKU、聚焦高端或細分領域 (例如美式足球釘鞋已成為美國第二大品牌)，
同時簡化工作流程，確保長期利潤率改善空間。 
投資建議 
考量歐美客流量逆風與促銷影響仍延續，我們維持 Under Armour「持有」
評等，目標價改採 FY28 每股淨值 1.6 倍推算，調整為 5.5 美元。 
投資風險 
上檔風險為消費較預期強勁與品牌重拾人氣。

焦點內容

1. 2Q FY27 (7-9 月；財年止於 3 月) 財報

### Page 2

美國 Under Armour

十一月 09, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  2

Under Armour 11 月 6 日法說會重點摘要 
 2Q FY27 (財年止於 3 月；7-9 月) 每股盈餘為 0.00 美元，符合預期。
營收 12.4 億美元，年減幅度擴大至 7%，反映全產品及全通路需求皆
轉差。受通路、地區組合不利與折扣力道加大影響，毛利率年減 0.3、
季減 7.1 個百分點至 47.0%，拖累營利率至僅 1.2%。 
 產品別營收：服飾 (佔總營收 71%)、鞋類 (19%) 及配件 (8%) 全面
惡化至年減 6%、11%、7%。 
 市場別營收：北美 (佔總營收 59%)、歐非中東 (23%) 及亞太平洋 
(13%) 分別年減 7%、9%、9%，符合公司指引。拉美 (4%) 年增
3%，為唯一成長市場。 
 通路別營收：批發業務 (佔總營收 58%) 年減 6%，再度轉弱，主因
全價商品銷售疲軟及部分分銷管道遇逆風。直接面對消費者 (DTC，
40%) 衰退擴大至 8%，反映客流量壓力導致直營店與電商業務分別
年減 7%、12%。 
 2Q FY27 庫存週轉天數年增 7%、但季減 17%至 149 天，仍高於過去
十年同期平均水準之 131 天，主因在途存貨較高。公司正透過與合作
工廠協商推遲部分發貨，同時考慮 2H FY27 從批發端去化庫存，但將
避開同業清倉甩賣的場所。 
 FY27 營收維持年減中個位數之展望，但基於油價和運價上漲、庫存
清倉與匯率不利等因素影響，將毛利率指引下調至 47.2-47.2% (前次
為 47.7-48.2%)，且年增 1.7-2.2 個百分點中約有 1.5 個百分點係來自
美國退稅挹注。加上組織轉型與重組費用認列，預計每股虧損 0.02
至每股盈餘 0.02 美元。 
 短期而言，管理層觀察到歐美客流量下滑與同業促銷力道加大風險，
預計 3Q FY27 營收將年減 8-9%；其中，北美及歐非中東將分別年減
低雙位數、高個位數，亞太約呈年持平。加上毛利率年增 0.3-0.5 個
百分點展望，3Q FY27 營運落後市場預期。

圖 6: 公司概況  圖 7: 服飾貢獻營收最大比重 
成立於 1996 年，Under Armour 為國際領導運動品牌，致力
提供專業運動服飾、鞋類及配件；其產品採用創新材料技術，
包括吸濕排汗、溫度控管、緩衝與布料永續利用等，目的在
創造差異化，並提升消費者品牌體驗。美洲為 FY26 營收主要
來源地區，佔比達 58%，其次分別為歐非中東 24%、亞太平
洋 14%、拉丁美洲 4%。

股價說明 等級 定義 
 增加持股 (OP) 對個股持正面看法，預期個股未來十二個月的表現超越凱基證券集團所追蹤的相關市場的總
報酬。 
 持有 (N) 對個股持中性看法，預期個股未來十二個月的表現符合凱基證券集團所追蹤的相關市場的總
報酬。 
 降低持股 (U) 對個股持負面看法，預期個股未來十二個月的表現低於凱基證券集團所追蹤的相關市場的總
報酬。 
 未評等 (NR) 凱基證券未對該個股加以評等。 
 受法規限制 
未評等 (R) 
受凱基證券集團內部政策和/或相關法令限制使凱基證券集團無法進行某些形式的資訊交
流，其中包括提供評等給投資人參考。 
  *總報酬 = (十二個月目標價-現價)/現價 
免責聲明

中航证券
研究所
发布 
淡。一级市场新品集中上市与二级市场成交缩量并存，构成本月较为突出的
运行特征，供给端扩容尚未同步转化为存量品种交易活跃度的提升。 
从内部结构来看，
10
月板块表现较上月有所切换。保障性租赁住房、
仓储物流和生态环保
REITs
 表现相对靠前，其中保障性租赁住房延续稳健
表现，仓储物流由前期调整转为修复；园区基础设施和新型基础设施
REITs
回调幅度相对较大，各类型板块间轮动持续性有限。个基上涨面有所扩大，
但不同板块及同类资产内部仍存在较为明显的分化。 
一级市场方面，本月共有
5
只
 REITs
 上市，覆盖保障性租赁住房、水
利设施、仓储物流和商业不动产四类资产。与此前月份单月新增产品相对较
少的情况相比，本月上市节奏有所加快，且新增基金并非集中于单一板块，
市场扩容呈现多类资产共同推进的特征。两只保障性租赁住房
REITs
 先后
上市，使住房保障领域成为本月扩容较为集中的方向，也为市场增加了以租
赁运营为基础的收益型资产。银华粤海水务水利
REIT
 上市，将水利设施发
电相关资产纳入公募
REITs
 市场，进一步拓展了水利资产的证券化范围，
并为盘活存量水利资产、支持新增水利投资提供了实践样本。中金菜鸟
REIT
 上市，为仓储物流板块补充了新的物流节点资产。华安锦江商业
REIT
则将商业不动产
REITs
 的底层业态延伸至酒店，为观察酒店资产的经营现
金流和估值定价提供了新的市场样本。整体来看，
REITs
 市场的持续扩容
已不再仅体现为基金数量增加，而是逐步体现为底层资产、现金流来源、区
域覆盖和原始权益人类型的同步丰富，进一步提升
REITs
 市场对不同类型
存量资产的承接能力。 
政策层面，制度推进有望与供给扩容形成协同。
10
月
 9
日，证监会就
公募基金运作管理办法及配套规则公开征求意见，拟进一步明确公募基金
投资
REITs
 的相关规则口径。同时，监管部门于
10
月
 19
日提出加快推进
REITs
 纳入沪深港通，为内地与香港市场互联互通明确了政策方向。如若
政策逐步落地，
REITs
 市场容量有望逐步扩大，市场可投资标的丰富度有
望进一步提升。

 合计有基金
 20
 只，总市值为

 66.70%
 ；涨跌情况方面，博
时津开产园
 REIT
 本月涨幅最大（
 +3.84%
 ），中金联东科创
 REIT
 本月跌幅最大（
 -
11.12%
 ）；交易数据方面，中信建投沈阳国际软件园
REIT
 月平均换手率最高（
1.04%
），
东吴苏园产业
 REIT
 月平均换手率最低（
 0.16%
）。板块层面，园区基础设施
REITs
 指
数本月下跌
 3.57%
，在
 8
个板块中排名第
 8
，板块内
 5
只基金实现上涨。  
10
月园区基础设施
 REITs
 板块
延续回调，但跌幅较
 9
月有所收窄，板块排名仍
居第
 8
位。
板块内部来看
，博时津开产园
 REIT
 、广发成都高投产业园
 REIT
、建信中
关村
 REIT
 等实现上涨，中金联东科创
 REIT
 、易方达广开产园
 REIT
 、中金亦庄产业
园
 REIT
 等调整幅度较大，板块内部走势仍有明显分化。交易活跃度方面，中信建投
沈阳国际软件园
 REIT
 月度换手继续处于板块较高水平，但较
 9
月有所下降；东吴苏
园产业
 REIT
 换手率仍处低位，资金参与热度尚未出现整体性回升。整体来看，本月
跌幅收窄或更多体现为前期调整后的局部修复，短期市场表现仍可能受到市场情绪、
流动性环境及基金估值消化进度等多重因素的综合影响。中长期来看，园区资产的结
构性分化格局尚未改变；未来城市更新项目与
 REITs
 发行范围的有机衔接，或将为板
块提供新的发展动能。其中，运营体系成熟、租约结构稳定且区位条件较优的项目有
望
持续受益
。

中航证券
研究所

三、
重要事件及市场动态  
重要事件  
1
、
公募基金运作规则拟修订，
 REITs
 投资比例适用口径进一步
明确 
10
月
 9
日，中国证监会就《公开募集证券投资基金运作管理办法（征求意见稿）》
及配套规则公开征求意见。征求意见稿将不动产投资信托基金纳入基金类别，并拟明
确基金财产投资
 REITs
 时适用证券投资相关比例要求。相关安排有助于厘清公募基金
投资
 REITs
 的规则口径，为公募资金参与
 REITs
 市场提供制度基础。目前文件仍处
于征求意见阶段，具体影响需待正式规则落地后进一步观察。  
2
、
证监会部署加快推进
 REITs
纳入沪深港通，市场开放预期进
一步增强  
10
月
 19
日，中国证监会主席吴清在
 2026
 金融街论坛年会上发表主题演讲，提
出
"
落实内地与香港市场
 '
五个协同
 '
合作新模式，加快推进人民币股票交易柜台和
REITs
 纳入沪深港通
 "
。相关部署为内地与香港
REITs
 市场互联互通提供了政策方向。
若后续落地，有望拓宽
 REITs
 投资者范围，提升跨境配置便利度，并对市场流动性和
定价效率形成积极影响。  
3
、
全国首单水利设施发电
 REITs
上市，水利资产证券化实现突
破 
10
月
 28
日，银华粤海水务水利
 REIT
 在深交所上市。该产品底层资产为飞来峡
水利枢纽、潮州供水枢纽发电相关特定资产及发电业务收费权，是全国首单水利设施
发电
 REITs
、全国首单省级水利
 REITs
，也是广东首单水利
 REITs
。募集资金拟用于
黄茅峡水库工程、环北部湾广东水资源配置工程等重大水利工程建设。该项目将存量
水利资产盘活与新增工程投资相衔接，为社会资本参与水利基础设施建设提供了新的
渠道，也拓展了公募
 REITs
 在水利领域的应用。

四、
核心观点  
10
月，中证
REITs
（收盘）指数下跌
0.84%
 ，中证
REITs
 全收益指数下跌
 0.37%
。
REITs
 市场整体延续调整态势，但表现仍优于主要股指。成交方面，成交规模环比与
同比均维持回落趋势，市场活跃度整体表现相对平淡。一级市场新品集中上市与二级

### Page 16

中航证券
研究所
发布 
市场成交缩量并存，构成本月较为突出的运行特征
 ，供给端扩容尚未同步转化
 为存量
品种交易活跃度的提升。  
从内部结构
来
看，
10
月板块
表现较上月有所切换
。保障性租赁住房、仓储物流和
生态环保
 REITs
 表现相对靠前，其中保障性租赁住房延续稳健表现，仓储物流由
 前期
调整转为修复；园区基础设施和新型基础设施
 REITs
 回调幅度相对较大
，各类型
板块
间
轮动持续性有限。个基上涨面有所扩大，但不同板块及同类资产内部仍存在较为明
显的分化。  
一级市场方面，
本
月共有
 5
只
 REITs
 上市
，
覆盖保障性租赁住房、水利设施、仓
储物流和商业不动产四类资产。与此前月份单月新增产品相对较少的情况相比，本月
上市节奏有所加快
，
且新增基金并非集中于单一板块，市场扩容呈现多类资产共同推
进的特征。两只保障性租赁住房
 REITs
 先后上市，使住房保障领域成为本月扩容较为
集中的方向，也为市场增加了以租赁运营为基础的收益型资产。银华粤海水务水利
REIT
 上市，将水利设施发电相关资产纳入公募
 REITs
 市场，进一步拓展了水利资产
的证券化范围，并为盘活存量水利资产、支持新增水利投资提供了实践样本。中金菜
鸟
 REIT
 上市，为仓储物流板块补充了新的物流节点资产。华安锦江商业
 REIT
 则将
商业不动产
 REITs
 的底层业态延伸至酒店，为观察酒店资产的经营现金流和估值定价
提供了新的市场样本。整体
 来
看，
REITs
 市场的持续扩容已不再仅体现为基金数量增
加，而是逐步体现为底层资产、现金流来源、区域覆盖和原始权益人类型的同步丰富
 ，
进一步
提升
 REITs
 市场对不同类型存量资产的承接能力
 。 
政策层面
，制度推进有望与供给扩容形成
 协同
。
10
月
 9
日，
证监会就公募基金运
作管理办法及配套规则公开征求意见，拟进一步明确公募基金投资
 REITs
 的相关规则
口径。同时，监管部门
于
 10
月
 19
日
提出加快推进
 REITs
 纳入沪深港通，为内地与香
港市场互联互通
明确
了政策方向。
如若政策逐步落地，
REITs
 市场容量
有望逐步
扩大
，
市场
可投资标的丰富度有望进一步提升
 。 
投资策略方面，建议继续以现金流稳定性和资产质量作为筛选基础，并结合市场
扩容后的资产谱系加强分类比较。
 近期新品上市数量较多，
 对于新上市基金，应重点
考察底层资产质量、发行估值、运营安排和现金流
 情况
；对于存量基金，则应持续跟
踪经营
与
分派兑现情况，在控制估值与流动性风险的基础上，适度关注基本面预期改
善带来的配置机会。长期来看，可重点关注以下方向：  
1.
交通基础设施
 REITs
 具有一定的垄断性和刚需属性，具备较强的稳定性。板块
内部因区域经济与产业结构差异呈现分化，部分路产车流量增长承压，而核心城市群
路产表现相对稳健，
 10
 月板块整体小幅调整，但内部表现并非一致走弱，区域位置、
路网结构和运营差异仍是
 个基
定价的重要依据。中长期看，交通强国建设和收费公路
相关制度完善，有助于为优质路产提供相对稳定的发展环境。建议优先关注具有区域
垄断地位、路网位置较为核心、车流量和通行费收入可预期性较强的项目。

### Page 17

中航证券
研究所
发布 
2.
仓储物流
 REITs
 板块
近期
有所修复。随着新品供给增加，区位条件、租户结构
和租约安排的重要性将更加突出
 ，同时，
 "
十五五
"
规划将现代流通体系和产业链供应
链安全作为重点，预计将有更多一线及新一线城市的枢纽节点项目入市，为市场注入
优质增量。建议重点关注位于核心物流节点、租户结构相对分散、续租能力较强且现
金流稳定的产品。  
3.
消费基础设施
 REITs
。
消费基础设施的收益表现取决于客流恢复
 、
客流转化、
租户组合、租金调整和主动运营能力。体验式业态占比高、运营调改能力强的购物中
心有望率先兑现盈利改善，估值
 存在
修复空间。后续在扩大内需和消费设施建设相关
政策支持下，具备成熟运营体系、业态调整能力和稳定品牌合作资源的商业项目更有
可能形成持续经营优势。  
4.
新型基础设施
 REITs
。
"
东数西算
"
、
"
数字经济
"
国家战略与
 AI
算力需求扩张共
振，数据中心、智算中心兼具政策红利、高成长性与稀缺赛道属性
 ，且行业
发展为数
据中心等资产提供
了
中长期需求基础。
考虑到
顶层设计已将数据中心纳入
 REITs
 资产
范围，
板块估值中枢有望维持在相对较高水平
 。建议优先关注合同质量较高、客户信
用较好、运营稳定性较强的项目
 。 
5.
园区基础设施
 REITs
。板块经过持续调整后，部分项目的估值压力有所释放。
结构上，厂房类资产依托制造业基本盘，或持续受益于先进制造业扩产与产业链自主
可控趋势；
 "
十五五
"
时期老旧街区、厂区改造任务明确，城市更新项目纳入
 REITs
 发
行范围后，园区存量资产的盘活路径进一步打通。具备优质区位、成熟运营能力与均
衡业态组合的园区资产，其估值修复空间值得重点关注。

五、
风险提示  
流动性不足风险
 ：部分
 REITs
 规模较小、投资者关注度低，可能存在二级市场交易
不活跃的情况。  
政策调控风险
：
REITs
 受行业政策影响较大，土地政策、税收政策、行业监管政策
等政策变化可能对
 REITs

## Document 404: Tracking Europe’s AI transition_ A new STOXX 600 earnings-call framework (2026-11-11)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Tracking Europe’s AI transition_ A new STOXX 600 earnings-call framework (2026-11-11).pdf
Report source: DB Research
Date: 2026-11-11
Pages: 16
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank 
Research Institute

Tracking Europe’s AI transition:  
A new STOXX 600 earnings-call 
framework

1 
Authors 
Michael Kirker 
Economist

November 11, 2026 
How quickly is AI moving from experimentation into everyday business, and 
where are companies reporting tangible gains? The answers matter for 
institutional investors and policymakers assessing productivity, operating 
efficiency and labour demand. Yet comparable evidence across Europe remains 
limited, making it difficult to track the pace and scale of the transition. 1 
We address this gap with a proprietary framework that turns what is said on 
STOXX 600 earnings call into three structured indicators: AI adoption 
intensity, reported productivity benefits, and the impact on workforces. 
Large language models analyse what management teams say about AI’s 
operational role, applying a common scoring framework across companies, 
sectors and countries. Our new approach provides a timely pan-European 
comparable tracker that captures not just whether AI is being mentioned, but its 
real-world impact as the adoption of the new technology unfolds. 
Key Takeaways: 
— Adoption is broadening, with signs of faster progress. AI-use scores rose 
gradually from a low base before strengthening over the past 12 months. The 
increase extends across sectors, although the pace and level of adoption 
remain uneven. 
— Workforce impacts remain limited in corporate disclosures. The 
framework does not show a broad negative workforce signal attributable to 
AI. The aggregate score remains close to the level of no impact on the labour 
force across all of our sample. However, this does not rule out AI displacing 
workers at a future date. 
— Geographic differences persist within the listed-company sample. Since 
2022, German companies in the STOXX 600 have generally recorded above-
average AI-use and reported productivity scores. Dutch and Spanish 
companies in the STOXX 600 have tended to score lower on AI use, although 
Spain has recently gone through a rapid catch up. 
— The technology J-Curve: The findings are consistent with an early stage of 
the AI transition. Companies report increasing adoption of the new 
technology, but transformational productivity gains and extensive workforce 
restructuring remain uncommon.

1 1 Our US team regularly publish on data from the USCB showing the impact of 
AI on businesses, and our German team have recently begun their own 
chartbook. In the UK, the Office for National Statistics publishes on the topic too.

### Page 2

Deutsche Bank AG Page 2

Methodology 
Sample period: Our sample begins in January 2022, providing a baseline before 
ChatGPT’s launch on 30 November 2022. AI in this framework covers both 
established and emerging technologies, so the launch of ChatGPT is a reference 
point rather than the start of all corporate AI adoption. 
Timing of observations: STOXX 600 companies report on different financial 
calendars and at quarterly or semi-annual intervals. Their earnings calls also mix 
discussion of completed activity with forward-looking guidance. 
We assign each transcript to the calendar quarter in which the earnings call takes 
place, regardless of the financial reporting period discussed. The resulting 
indicators therefore track when information about AI use and its operational 
impacts becomes public rather than when implementation or the reported effect 
occurred. This provides a common dating convention across the sample while 
retaining differences in reporting frequency and disclosure timing.

Figure 1: Coverage of the STOXX 600 earnings calls

Source: Deutsche Bank Research. Notes: Share of the STOXX 600 is based on the current company weight in the index. Data collection for the current quarter (Q4 2026) is incomplete 
as some companies are still to report

Constructing the STOXX 600 AI corpus: We use a specialised AI transcription 
engine to process earnings calls from STOXX 600 companies, restricting the 
analysis to calls for which we obtain complete transcripts of sufficient accuracy. 
In Q3 2026, the latest full quarter, the dataset covered more than 500 companies, 
representing approximately 90% of the index’s current market capitalisation 
weights (Figure 1). In the current quarter (Q4) earnings calls are still taking place. 
Nearly 250 companies with an equivalent weight to around c.60% of the STOXX 
index having already reported. 
Historical coverage typically ranges from 300 to 500 companies and represents 
more than 80% of the index’s current market-capitalisation weights. Coverage 
peaks twice a year because some European companies report semi-annually.  
11 November 2026 
Focus Europe

### Page 3

Deutsche Bank AG Page 3

### Page 4

Deutsche Bank AG Page 4

For examples of the AI’s scoring and justifications, see the tables presented in the 
Annex at the end of this report. 
Summary statistics 
Figure 2 shows the distribution of scores across all earnings-call transcripts in the 
historical sample, without weighting for company size or reporting frequency. 
These are transcript-level distributions rather than shares of companies, and they 
combine early and recent observations. They provide context for the quarterly 
indicators: 
• AI use score: Approximately 60% of earnings call transcripts do not 
discuss AI use. Among the minority that do discuss it, most fall into the 
“Active implementation” range (a score of 4-6 out of 10). 
• AI’s productivity impact: Nearly 70% of earnings calls do not mention a 
tangible AI-related productivity benefit. Among calls that do, scores are 
generally in the medium-impact band, indicating notable operational 
improvements. Transformational gains, scored at 8 or above out of 10, 
are rarely reported. 
• AI’s labour impact: Only around 10% of earnings calls discuss AI-related 
workforce adjustments. Reported changes typically receive scores of 1–
2 in magnitude on the −5 to +5 scale, with a slight tilt towards hiring and 
upskilling rather than redundancies.

Figure 2: Raw distribution of scores across the entire sample

Source: Deutsche Bank Research. Note: Results are based on the raw number of transcripts, and are independent of firm size, and biased towards firms reporting more often.

Taken together, the distributions are consistent with an early adoption phase: 
companies discuss AI implementation more often than transformational 
productivity gains or extensive workforce restructuring. A technology J-curve 
offers one interpretation of this pattern, with investment and organisational 
adaptation preceding broader benefits.  
This J-curve pattern is strongly corroborated by external micro-data, such as the 
Banco de España’s business activity survey (EBAE) of Spanish firms, which 
11 November 2026 
Focus Europe

### Page 5

Deutsche Bank AG Page 5

similarly indicates that AI has a more immediate impact on upfront investment 
and productivity than on headcount. 
AI use: A broad-based rise, with signs of faster recent adoption 
The new AI-use indicator points to a broadening transition across STOXX 600 
companies, with early signs that adoption has accelerated recently. Scores rose 
only gradually from 2022 and 2024, but the latest observations suggest a 
stronger pace across industries and country groups.  
The rise is broad-based rather than confined to one sector. Technology has led 
much of the sample, while financials show tentative recent outperformance and 
utilities have caught up from a low starting point. Among the large country groups, 
German companies have persistently recorded above-average scores.  
AI use across European industries 
To trace sectoral dispersion, we group companies by STOXX industry 
classification and calculate a weighted average of their scores for each calendar 
quarter, using their latest STOXX 600 index weights. 2  
We focus on four sectors spanning different business models: financials, the 
largest services sector; industrials, the largest physical-assets sector; technology, 
a likely early adopter; and utilities, an infrastructure-heavy sector where adoption 
may proceed more slowly.  
Figure 3 presents the AI use score for these four key industries alongside the 
range for all industry scores (represented by the shaded area). 
• The AI use score is generally low over time but is gradually rising from 
around 1 point in 2022 to around 3 in 2026. 
• AI-use scores are rising across industries, but differences between 
sectors are widening. The common direction therefore points to 
broadening adoption rather than uniform progress: sectors can 
participate in the transition while remaining at different levels of use. 
In terms of the industries of interest: 
• Technology’s early lead has narrowed. The sector’s AI-use score was 
near the top of the industry range through to 2024 but has recently 
plateaued as the broader STOXX 600 average caught up during 2025–
26.3 
• Financials and industrials broadly track the index average. Both sectors 
have remained close to the aggregate AI-use score over history, with 
financials generally a little below average, and industrials a little above. 
But financials show tentative signs of moving above average in recent 
quarters.

Utilities.

• Utilities have caught up from a low base. After recording lower AI-use 
scores than other sectors for much of the sample, utilities converged 
towards the STOXX 600 average during 2026. The result shows that the 
rise in reported adoption extends beyond the sectors most closely 
associated with AI.

Figure 3: AI use score (0-10) by industry

Source: Deutsche Bank Research. Notes: The value for each industry is computed as a weighted average of all firms within the industry where the weights are derived from the current 
STOXX 600 share of each company. The markers denote the readings for the current incomplete quarter

As a cross-check, our findings align with the European Commission’s October 
Eurobarometer Flash survey of MSEs and Mid-Caps and the Eurostat annual ICT 
surveys, which show low overall intensity but a similar expansion in service- and 
tech-related sectors.  
AI use across countries 
Companies in the STOXX 600 tend to be large multi-nationals. That makes it 
difficult to pin down where physically the AI transformation is taking place within 
their organisation. Instead, our country comparison is done using two 
classifications of a company’s country: 
• Country of domicile: AI is a transformation technology, and this type of 
change is likely to be driven in organisation from the top down. We 
therefore consider a company’s country as that in which it is domiciled 
(which is likely to be where the headquarters is also). 
• Country of primary listing: While companies can generally list in other 
countries, there tends to be a home bias in their choice of exchange.  
Figure 4 presents the AI use intensity metric by both of these measures of country 
(the company’s domicile and primary exchange listing). Again, the shaded area 
shows the range across all classifications, with the largest countries/exchanges 
singled out. 
German-domiciled companies and the Xetra-listed (Frankfurt stock exchange) 
group record above-average AI-use scores in most quarters since 2022.  
Spain’s AI-use score has risen noticeably over recent quarters and now exceeds 
the aggregate average, reversing its earlier below-average position.  
11 November 2026
Focus Europe

### Page 7

Deutsche Bank AG Page 7

AI-use scores rise across country groups, with indications of faster growth from 
2025. The shared direction suggests a broad European listed-company trend, 
even though differences in score levels and timing persist between countries.

Figure 4: AI use intensity (0-10) by country

Source: Deutsche Bank Research. Notes: The markers denote the readings for the current incomplete quarter

As a geographical cross-check, our index matches the broad trends of the 
Eurostat ICT annual survey, which places Germany as a clear frontrunner among 
its large peers. But some differences do exist. For instance, Eurostat shows the 
Netherlands with a very high AI adoption. As Figure 5 demonstrates, there is a 
positive correlation between the AI use score metric derived here, and that from 
Eurostat, even though the Eurostat data measures if firms are adopting AI, and 
our new use measure also incorporates the concept of how intensively firms are 
using AI.

AI productivity impact: Reported benefits are rising, but 
transformational gains remain uncommon 
Higher intensity of use does not necessarily mean firms are benefiting more from 
AI. But our AI productivity impact metric does show a similar pattern to our AI 
usage metric, although it tends to be slightly lower, indicating that companies 
may not yet be reaping the full rewards for their AI investment. 
Financials stand out more clearly on reported productivity benefits than on AI-use 
intensity, while German companies again record relatively strong scores, but only 
in more recent history.

Figure 6: AI's impact on productivity (0-10) by industry

Source: Deutsche Bank Research. Notes: The markers denote the readings for the current incomplete quarter

AI productivity impact across European industries 
Reported AI-productivity benefits have become more prominent across 
industries. Figure 6 shows scores rising gradually during 2022–25, followed by 
11 November 2026

indications of a faster increase from late 2025. The profile resembles the AI-use 
trend in Figure 4, suggesting that the stronger adoption narrative is accompanied 
by more discussion of operational gains. 
The aggregate trajectories of the AI use and AI productivity impact scores are 
similar, but sector rankings differ. Technology’s early relative strength in AI use is 
not matched by its productivity score, while financials report above-average 
productivity benefits despite more modest use scores. 
AI productivity impact across countries 
Figure 7 plots the self-report impact of AI on company productivity by country. 
Between 2022-23, German companies recorded average productivity impact, 
even though their AI use score was relatively high. However more recently, their 
AI impact score has persistently been above average. The UK grouping shows 
persistent early outperformance in AI impact during 2022–23, followed by 
convergence towards the aggregate average.

AI labour force impact: On net, AI may be benefiting 
employment, for now… 
Across industries and countries, the recorded score for AI’s impact on the labour 
force remains relatively small. On average, the reported impact on companies’ 
employment has been fairly neutral, with a marginal negative bias over the last 
year or so.

Figure 9: AI's impact on the labour force (-5 to +5) by industry

Source: Deutsche Bank Research. Notes: The markers denote the readings for the current incomplete quarter. Negative (positive) scores are associated with job losses (gains) 
associated with a company's AI use.

11 November 2026 
Focus Europe

### Page 11

Deutsche Bank AG Page 11

AI labour force impact across European industries 
Despite rising productivity, AI has not triggered the negative employment shock 
feared by the public. Aggregate labour impact scores across nearly all sectors 
hover near zero, with a slightly positive balance over history, and a slightly 
negative balance more recently in 2025-26 (Figure 9). But overall the magnitude 
of AI’s impact on labour remains low. For the STOXX 600 aggregate average, the 
score’s maximum deviation from 0 (no impact) is 0.43 on our 0-5 scale. 
AI labour force impact across countries 
Workforce-impact scores are also small across the main country groupings, with 
most countries slowing a slight negative tilt in recent years (Figure 10).

Figure 10: AI impact on the labour force (-5 to +5) by country

Source: Deutsche Bank Research. Notes: The markers denote the readings for the current incomplete quarter. Negative (positive) scores are associated with job losses (gains) 
associated with a company's AI use.

The lack of significant impact so far from AI on companies’ labour forces so far is 
consistent with results from our previous work looking at the other side of the 
labour market. Data from our dbDataInsights household survey showed that 
workers across Europe reported a fairly low level of fear about losing their job to 
AI over the next year. The analysis in that report found results that were more 
consistent with workers adapting to the new AI world by upskilling and 
11 November 2026 
Focus Europe

### Page 12

Deutsche Bank AG Page 12

demanding higher wages. Those results would not be consistent with a world in 
which AI was actively replacing labour. 
These findings of a low impact on labour are highly consistent with recent 
academic research (e.g., Yotzov et al., 2026), which found that nearly half of 
surveyed UK and German firms reported absolutely no impact on headcount from 
AI over the past three years, 
Conclusion 
The newly developed framework provides a recurring way to monitor company 
disclosures about AI’s impact on productivity and labour. The central finding of 
this report is a broadening AI transition rather than clear evidence of an 
economy-wide productivity breakthrough. The new metrics indicate that AI use 
remains limited but is rising across the European countries and industries 
represented in the sample. Companies report low but growing productivity gains 
and little aggregate workforce impact so far. This pattern is consistent with a 
technology J-curve, with firms still integrating the new technology, rather than 
proof that this trajectory will unfold. Limited reported workforce effects today do 
not rule out subsequent layoffs or wider organisational restructuring. 
Quarterly updates to this framework will track Europe’s AI transition and provide 
timely signals of whether companies are reporting more substantial productivity 
and employment effects. 
Beyond AI, the framework could be adapted to extract signals about employment 
decisions, investment decisions and the general economic outlook from earnings 
calls. These potential extensions could form the basis of alternative nowcasting 
models for important macroeconomic data; they are applications that will be 
explored in future work.

11 November 2026 
Focus Europe

By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 8 Nov 2026

### Page 2

Last Thursday’s selloff in the stock market was an AI selloff. Investors
dumped companies that build, wire, and power data centers, following the
Democrats' sweep of Congress amid voter hostility toward new facilities.
Stocks recovered some ground on Friday. October payrolls rose by
165,000, beating expectations of 65,000. Still, the S&P 500 ended the
week down 2.7%, and Information Technology (MW) fell 6.9% (chart).
Energy (OW) was the worst-performing sector this week, down 11.9%, for

a different reason. Oil prices tumbled on hopes of a US-Iran peace deal
following the midterm elections. We are retaining our overweight
recommendation on Energy as a hedge against re-escalation. Consumer
Discretionary (UW) and Communication Services (MW) led the week, up
2.7% and 2.3%.
The market is pricing the AI backlash as a roadblock. We see a speed
bump. Investors have already cut the multiples of the build-out’s suppliers.
Analysts have yet to cut their earnings estimates. That is the next test. We

are not changing any of our sector weights. Here’s more on Information
Technology, Industrials, and Utilities:
(1) Information Technology. The selloff hit the build-out’s hardware
suppliers the hardest. Semiconductor Equipment fell 14.5% this week,
followed by Semiconductors (-10.9%) and Communication Equipment
(-9.4%) (chart).

### Page 3

Semiconductor Equipment is the clearest case of a speed bump priced as
a roadblock. The industry’s stock price index is 27% below its 200-day
moving average (chart). Yet its forward earnings and forward profit margin
are at record highs. Its forward P/E has dropped to 21.2 from about 52.3
in June.
Analysts keep raising their estimates for Semiconductor Equipment
stocks. Collectively, they expect the industry’s earnings to grow 35.4% this
year and 51.5% next year (chart). Their consensus earnings estimate for

2027 has risen steadily all year. Semiconductor Equipment is one of only
two IT industries for which analysts expect faster growth next year than
this year.

### Page 4

Valuations are far from tech-bubble extremes. IT and Communication
Services together account for 47.6% of the S&P 500’s market
capitalization and a nearly identical 47.1% of its forward earnings (chart).
In 2000, when valuations were at tech-bubble extremes, their market-cap
share far exceeded their earnings share.
Tech's forward P/E has fallen to 17.3, and Semiconductors' forward P/E is
down to 12.0. That already discounts some earnings disappointment. A

modest cut in estimates is probably priced in. A deep one is not. That’s
why we remain at a market weight rating.
(2) Industrials. The AI-exposed industries led Industrials lower this week.
Electrical Components & Equipment fell 9.1%, and Construction

### Page 5

Machinery & Heavy Trucks fell 7.8% (chart). Passenger Airlines, Industrial
Conglomerates, and Industrial Machinery all rose.
Electrical Components shows the same disconnect as Semiconductor
Equipment. Its stock price index is 13.2% below its 200-day moving
average. Yet its forward revenues and forward earnings are at record
highs, and its forward profit margin rose to a record 18.2% (chart).
Analysts expect the industry’s earnings to grow 20.5% this year and

19.1% next year. Its forward P/E has fallen to 23.0 from over 30.
The Construction Machinery & Heavy Trucks index is trading at 26%
below its 200-day moving average, even as its forward earnings have
risen to a record high. Elsewhere, Rail Transportation is up 24.4% ytd.
Analysts have trimmed the sector’s 2027 earnings growth estimate to

### Page 6

15.8%, from 19.0% in July. That is still strong growth, and it supports our
overweight rating.
(3) Utilities. The gap between this sector’s best and worst performing
industries is enormous (chart). The Independent Power Producers &
Energy Traders industry stock price index is down 18.2% ytd, including a
7.8% drop this week. Water Utilities is up 15.2% ytd, while the Utilities
sector as a whole is up just 1.0% ytd.
IPPs sell power to data centers, and investors are pricing out that

premium before analysts do. The industry’s forward earnings is up 8.4%
ytd, while its stock price index is down 18.2% (chart). Its forward P/E has
fallen to 10.0, and its forward profit margin is at a record high.

### Page 7

Analysts are starting to follow investors’ lead. They cut their 2026 revenue
growth estimate for IPPs to 15.7% from 21.6% over the summer (chart).
But they still expect IPP earnings to grow 12.0% next year. The low
valuation suggests that investors expect more estimate cuts.
We downgraded Utilities to market weight on October 2 due to rising bond
yields. The data center premium coming out of the IPPs gives us a second
reason. We remain at a market weight rating.
